# Research: subset-b-000599

Grouped research for ceph-client Linux Devicetree PHY binding YAML schemas. Each section preserves the source path and is wrapped for deterministic reconciliation into source-tree-aligned per-file reports. The source schemas were parsed as YAML and summarized from their validation contracts, examples, compatible strings, child-node rules, resource dependencies, and in-tree driver/DTS match signals.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,cygnus-pcie-phy.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,cygnus-pcie-phy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Broadcom Cygnus PCIe PHY`, a PCIe PHY binding. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence and covers 1 compatible token: `brcm,cygnus-pcie-phy`. Top-level properties are `$nodename`, `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#address-cells`, `#phy-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `reg`. The binding acts as a parent provider with addressable child PHY/lane nodes. Provider/cell contracts are `#address-cells=const 1`, `#size-cells=const 0`, `#phy-cells=const 0`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `#address-cells` enables addressable child PHY, lane, or port nodes; `#size-cells` enables addressable child PHY, lane, or port nodes. Patterned child schemas are `^pcie-phy@[0-9]+$`; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `brcm,cygnus-pcie-phy`, `1`, `0`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Ray Jui <ray.jui@broadcom.com>, Scott Branden <scott.branden@broadcom.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/broadcom/phy-bcm-cygnus-pcie.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/broadcom/bcm-cygnus.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 5 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,cygnus-pcie-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,cygnus-pcie-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,cygnus-pcie-phy.yaml -->

In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/broadcom/bcm-nsp.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/broadcom/bcm63138.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/broadcom/bcm7445.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/broadcom/northstar2/ns2.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/mips/boot/dts/brcm/bcm7346.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/mips/boot/dts/brcm/bcm7360.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/mips/boot/dts/brcm/bcm7362.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/mips/boot/dts/brcm/bcm7425.dtsi`, and 1 more. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 6 top-level properties and 11 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,sata-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,sata-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,sr-pcie-phy.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,sr-pcie-phy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Broadcom Stingray PCIe PHY`, a PCIe PHY binding. For PAXB based root complex, one can have a configuration of up to 8 PHYs. PHY index goes from 0 to 7. For the internal PAXC based root complex, PHY index is always 8. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `brcm,sr-pcie-phy`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#phy-cells`, `brcm,sr-cdru`, `brcm,sr-mhb`. Top-level required properties are none declared at the top level; required properties discovered across nested schemas include none. The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 1`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `brcm,sr-pcie-phy`, `1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Ray Jui <ray.jui@broadcom.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/broadcom/phy-bcm-sr-pcie.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/broadcom/stingray/stingray-pcie.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,sr-pcie-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,sr-pcie-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/brcm,sr-pcie-phy.yaml -->

Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/cadence/cdns-dphy-rx.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-am62-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-am62a-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-am62p-j722s-common-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-j721e-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-j721s2-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-j722s-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-j784s4-j742s2-main-common.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/cdns,dphy-rx.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/cdns,dphy-rx.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/cdns,dphy.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/cdns,dphy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Cadence DPHY`, a MIPI D-PHY, CSI, or DSI PHY binding. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 2 compatible tokens: `cdns,dphy`, `ti,j721e-dphy`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#phy-cells`, `power-domains`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#phy-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`. The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 0`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `clock-names` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `power-domains` links the PHY to runtime PM domain providers. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `cdns,dphy`, `ti,j721e-dphy`, `psm`, `pll_ref`, `0`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Pratyush Yadav <pratyush@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/cadence/cdns-dphy-rx.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/cadence/cdns-dphy.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-am62-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-am62a-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-am62p-j722s-common-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-j721e-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-j721s2-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-j722s-main.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-j784s4-j742s2-main-common.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 6 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/cdns,dphy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/cdns,dphy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/fsl,imx8mp-hdmi-phy.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/fsl,imx8mp-hdmi-phy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Freescale i.MX8MP HDMI PHY`, a display PHY binding. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 1 compatible token: `fsl,imx8mp-hdmi-phy`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#phy-cells`, `power-domains`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#phy-cells`, `power-domains`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#clock-cells`, `#phy-cells`, `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `power-domains`, `reg`. The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 0`, `#clock-cells=const 0`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `clock-names` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `power-domains` links the PHY to runtime PM domain providers. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`, `maxItems=2`; selected enum/const literals include `fsl,imx8mp-hdmi-phy`, `0`, `apb`, `ref`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Lucas Stach <l.stach@pengutronix.de>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/freescale/phy-fsl-samsung-hdmi.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mp.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 7 top-level properties and 7 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/fsl,imx8mp-hdmi-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/fsl,imx8mp-hdmi-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/fsl,imx8mp-hdmi-phy.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/fsl,imx8qm-lvds-phy.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/fsl,imx8qm-lvds-phy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Mixel LVDS PHY for Freescale i.MX8qm SoC`, a display PHY binding. The Mixel LVDS PHY IP block is found on Freescale i.MX8qm SoC. It converts two groups of four 7/10 bits of CMOS data into two groups of four data lanes of LVDS data streams. A phase-locked transmit clock is transmitted in parallel with each group of data streams over a fifth LVDS link. Every cycle of the transmit clock, 56/80 bits of input data are sampled and transmitted through the two groups of LVDS data streams. Together with the transmit clocks, the two groups of LVDS data streams form two LVDS channels. The Mixel LVDS PHY found on Freescale i.MX8qm SoC is controlled by Control and Status Registers(CSR) module in the SoC. The CSR module, as a system controller, contains the PHY's registers. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 2 compatible tokens: `fsl,imx8qm-lvds-phy`, `mixel,28fdsoi-lvds-1250-8ch-tx-pll`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `power-domains`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `power-domains`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `power-domains`. The binding acts as a simple provider node referenced by PHY consumers. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 1`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `power-domains` links the PHY to runtime PM domain providers. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `fsl,imx8qm-lvds-phy`, `mixel,28fdsoi-lvds-1250-8ch-tx-pll`, `1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Liu Ying <victor.liu@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Patterned child schemas are `^phy@[0-7]$`; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minimum=0`, `maximum=7`; selected enum/const literals include `fsl,lynx-28g`, `1`, `0`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Ioana Ciornei <ioana.ciornei@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/freescale/phy-fsl-lynx-28g.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/fsl-lx2160a.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/fsl,lynx-28g.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/fsl,lynx-28g.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 7 top-level properties and 7 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/hisilicon,hi3798cv200-combphy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `HiSilicon STB PCIE/SATA/USB3 PHY`, a PCIe PHY binding. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `hisilicon,hi3798cv200-combphy`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `resets`, `hisilicon,fixed-mode`, `hisilicon,mode-select-bits`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `resets`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `hisilicon,fixed-mode`, `hisilicon,mode-select-bits`, `reg`, `resets`. The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 1`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `resets` models reset controller lines. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Mauro Carvalho Chehab <mchehab+huawei@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32-array`. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/hisilicon/phy-hi3670-pcie.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 7 top-level properties and 7 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/hisilicon,phy-hi3670-pcie.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/hisilicon,phy-hi3670-pcie.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/hisilicon,phy-hi3670-pcie.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/intel,lgm-emmc-phy.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/intel,lgm-emmc-phy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Intel Lightning Mountain(LGM) eMMC PHY`, a storage PHY binding. Bindings for eMMC PHY on Intel's Lightning Mountain SoC, syscon node is used to reference the base address of eMMC phy registers. The eMMC PHY node should be the child of a syscon node with the required property: - compatible: Should be one of the following: "intel,lgm-syscon", "syscon" - reg: maxItems: 1 It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 2 compatible tokens: `intel,lgm-emmc-phy`, `intel,keembay-emmc-phy`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `#phy-cells`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`. Top-level required properties are `#phy-cells`, `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`. The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 0`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `clock-names` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `intel,lgm-emmc-phy`, `intel,keembay-emmc-phy`, `0`, `emmcclk`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Ramuthevar Vadivel Murugan <vadivel.muruganx.ramuthevar@linux.intel.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/intel/phy-intel-keembay-emmc.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/intel/phy-intel-lgm-emmc.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 2 example blocks for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/intel,lgm-emmc-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/intel,lgm-emmc-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/intel,lgm-emmc-phy.yaml -->

The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,armada-3700-utmi-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,armada-3700-utmi-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,armada-375-usb-cluster.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,armada-375-usb-cluster.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Armada 375 USB Cluster`, a USB PHY or USB dual-role PHY binding. Control register for the Armada 375 USB cluster, managing USB2 and USB3 features. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `marvell,armada-375-usb-cluster`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#phy-cells`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#phy-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `compatible`, `reg`. The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 1`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `marvell,armada-375-usb-cluster`, `1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Andrew Lunn <andrew@lunn.ch>, Gregory Clement <gregory.clement@bootlin.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/marvell/phy-armada375-usb2.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/marvell/armada-375.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 3 top-level properties and 3 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,armada-375-usb-cluster.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,armada-375-usb-cluster.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Antoine Tenart <atenart@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/marvell/phy-berlin-usb.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/synaptics/berlin2cd.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/synaptics/berlin2q.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,berlin2-usb-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,berlin2-usb-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,berlin2-usb-phy.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,comphy-cp110.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,comphy-cp110.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Marvell MVEBU COMPHY Controller`, a multi-protocol SerDes/COMPHY binding. COMPHY controllers can be found on the following Marvell MVEBU SoCs: * Armada 7k/8k (on the CP110) * Armada 3700 It provides a number of shared PHYs used by various interfaces (network, SATA, USB, PCIe...). It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 2 compatible tokens: `marvell,comphy-cp110`, `marvell,comphy-a3700`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `marvell,system-controller`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `compatible`, `marvell,system-controller`, `reg`, `reg-names`. The binding acts as a parent provider with addressable child PHY/lane nodes. Provider/cell contracts are `#address-cells=const 1`, `#size-cells=const 0`, `#phy-cells=const 1`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `#address-cells` enables addressable child PHY, lane, or port nodes; `#size-cells` enables addressable child PHY, lane, or port nodes; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `clock-names` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks. Patterned child schemas are `^phy@[0-5]$`; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `minItems=1`, `maxItems=3`, `maximum=5`, `maxItems=1`, `minItems=3`; selected enum/const literals include `marvell,comphy-cp110`, `marvell,comphy-a3700`, `comphy`, `lane1_pcie_gbe`, `lane0_usb3_gbe`, `lane2_sata_usb3`, `1`, `0`, `xtal`, `mg_clk`, `mg_core_clk`, `axi_clk`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Miquel Raynal <miquel.raynal@bootlin.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/marvell/phy-mvebu-a3700-comphy.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/marvell/phy-mvebu-cp110-comphy.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/marvell/armada-37xx.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/marvell/armada-cp11x.dtsi`. The file includes 2 example blocks for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 8 top-level properties and 10 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,comphy-cp110.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,comphy-cp110.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Andrew Lunn <andrew@lunn.ch>, Gregory Clement <gregory.clement@bootlin.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/marvell/phy-mvebu-sata.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/marvell/dove.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/marvell/kirkwood.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,mvebu-sata-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,mvebu-sata-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/marvell,mvebu-sata-phy.yaml -->

Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `#phy-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `reg`. The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 0`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `resets` models reset controller lines. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,mt7621-pci-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,mt7621-pci-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,mt7621-pci-phy.yaml -->
