# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,mt7628-usbphy.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,mt7628-usbphy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Mediatek/Ralink USB PHY`, a USB PHY or USB dual-role PHY binding. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 3 compatible tokens: `mediatek,mt7620-usbphy`, `mediatek,mt7628-usbphy`, `ralink,rt3352-usbphy`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#phy-cells`, `ralink,sysctl`, `resets`, `reset-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `#phy-cells`, `ralink,sysctl`, `resets`, `reset-names`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `compatible`, `ralink,sysctl`, `reg`, `reset-names`, `resets`. The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 0`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `resets` models reset controller lines; `reset-names` models reset controller lines. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `mediatek,mt7620-usbphy`, `mediatek,mt7628-usbphy`, `ralink,rt3352-usbphy`, `0`, `host`, `device`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Sergio Paracuellos <sergio.paracuellos@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/ralink/phy-ralink-usb.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/mips/boot/dts/ralink/mt7628a.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 6 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,mt7628-usbphy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,mt7628-usbphy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,tphy.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,tphy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MediaTek T-PHY Controller`, a Linux PHY provider binding. The T-PHY controller supports physical layer functionality for a number of controllers on MediaTek SoCs, includes USB2.0, USB3.0, PCIe and SATA. Layout differences of banks between T-PHY V1 (mt8173/mt2701) and T-PHY V2 (mt2712) / V3 (mt8195) when works on USB mode: ----------------------------------- Version 1: port offset bank shared 0x0000 SPLLC 0x0100 FMREG u2 port0 0x0800 U2PHY_COM u3 port0 0x0900 U3PHYD 0x0a00 U3PHYD_BANK2 0x0b00 U3PHYA 0x0c00 U3PHYA_DA u2 port1 0x1000 U2PHY_COM u3 port1 0x1100 U3PHYD 0x1200 U3PHYD_BANK2 0x1300 U3PHYA 0x1400 U3PHYA_DA u2 port2 0x1800 U2PHY_COM ... Version 2/3: port offset bank u2 port0 0x0000 MISC 0x0100 FMREG 0x0300 U2PHY_COM u3 port0 0x0700 SPLLC 0x0800 CHIP 0x0900 U3... It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

Top-level properties are `$nodename`, `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `ranges`, `mediatek,src-ref-clk-mhz`, `mediatek,src-coef`, `power-domains`. The binding acts as a parent provider with addressable child PHY/lane nodes. Provider/cell contracts are `#address-cells=enum [1, 2]`, `#size-cells=enum [1, 2]`, `#phy-cells=const 1`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `#address-cells` enables addressable child PHY, lane, or port nodes; `#size-cells` enables addressable child PHY, lane, or port nodes; `power-domains` links the PHY to runtime PM domain providers. Patterned child schemas are `^(usb|pcie|sata)-phy@[0-9a-f]+$`; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=1`, `minimum=1`, `maximum=7`, `maximum=31`, `maximum=15`, `maximum=3`; selected enum/const literals include `mediatek,mt2701-tphy`, `mediatek,mt7623-tphy`, `mediatek,mt7622-tphy`, `mediatek,mt8516-tphy`, `mediatek,generic-tphy-v1`, `mediatek,mt2712-tphy`, `mediatek,mt6893-tphy`, `mediatek,mt7629-tphy`, `mediatek,mt7981-tphy`, `mediatek,mt7986-tphy`, `mediatek,mt8183-tphy`, `mediatek,mt8186-tphy`, `mediatek,mt8192-tphy`, `mediatek,mt8365-tphy`, `mediatek,generic-tphy-v2`, `mediatek,mt8188-tphy`, `mediatek,mt8195-tphy`, `mediatek,generic-tphy-v3`, `mediatek,mt2701-u3phy`, `mediatek,mt2712-u3phy`, and 10 more.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Chunfeng Yun <chunfeng.yun@mediatek.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/mediatek/phy-mtk-tphy.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/mediatek/mt2701.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7623.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7629.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt2712e.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7622.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7981b.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7986a.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt7988a.dtsi`, and 4 more. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 9 top-level properties and 23 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,tphy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mediatek,tphy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/microchip,lan966x-serdes.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/microchip,lan966x-serdes.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Microchip Lan966x Serdes controller`, a multi-protocol SerDes/COMPHY binding. Lan966x has 7 interfaces, consisting of 2 copper transceivers(CU), 3 SERDES6G and 2 RGMII interfaces. Two of the SERDES6G support QSGMII. Also it has 8 logical Ethernet ports which can be connected to these interfaces. The Serdes controller will allow to configure these interfaces and allows to "mux" the interfaces to different ports. For simple selection of the interface that is used with a port, the following macros are defined CU(X), SERDES6G(X), RGMII(X). Where X is a number that represents the index of that interface type. For example CU(1) means use interface copper transceivers 1. SERDES6G(2) means use interface SerDes 2. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#phy-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `compatible`, `reg`. The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 2`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. **Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/microchip,sparx5-serdes.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Microchip Sparx5 Serdes controller`, a multi-protocol SerDes/COMPHY binding. The Sparx5 SERDES interfaces share the same basic functionality, but support different operating modes and line rates. The following list lists the SERDES features: * RX Adaptive Decision Feedback Equalizer (DFE) * Programmable continuous time linear equalizer (CTLE) * Rx variable gain control * Rx built-in fault detector (loss-of-lock/loss-of-signal) * Adjustable tx de-emphasis (FFE) * Tx output amplitude control * Supports rx eye monitor * Multiple loopback modes * Prbs generator and checker * Polarity inversion control SERDES6G: The SERDES6G is a high-speed SERDES interface, which can operate at the following data rates: * 100 Mbps (100BASE-FX) * 1.25 Gbps (SGMII/1000BASE-X/1000BASE-KX) * 3.125 Gbps (2.5G... The binding acts as a MMIO or bus-addressed PHY provider node. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 1`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `minItems=1`, `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `microchip,sparx5-serdes`, `microchip,lan9691-serdes`, `microchip,lan9698-serdes`, `microchip,lan9696-serdes`, `microchip,lan9694-serdes`, `microchip,lan9693-serdes`, `microchip,lan9692-serdes`, `1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Steen Hegelund <steen.hegelund@microchip.com>, Daniel Machon <daniel.machon@microchip.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/microchip/sparx5_serdes.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/microchip/lan9691.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/microchip/sparx5.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/microchip,sparx5-serdes.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/microchip,sparx5-serdes.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mscc,vsc7514-serdes.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mscc,vsc7514-serdes.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Microsemi Ocelot SerDes muxing`, a multi-protocol SerDes/COMPHY binding. On Microsemi Ocelot, there is a handful of registers in HSIO address space for setting up the SerDes to switch port muxing. A SerDes X can be "muxed" to work with switch port Y or Z for example. One specific SerDes can also be used as a PCIe interface. Hence, a SerDes represents an interface, be it an Ethernet or a PCIe one. There are two kinds of SerDes: SERDES1G supports 10/100Mbps in half/full-duplex and 1000Mbps in full-duplex mode while SERDES6G supports 10/100Mbps in half/full-duplex and 1000/2500Mbps in full-duplex mode. Also, SERDES6G number (aka "macro") 0 is the only interface supporting QSGMII. This is a child of the HSIO syscon ("mscc,ocelot-hsio", see Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mips/mscc.... It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

The binding acts as a simple provider node referenced by PHY consumers. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 2`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected; selected enum/const literals include `mscc,vsc7514-serdes`, `2`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Alexandre Belloni <alexandre.belloni@bootlin.com>, UNGLinuxDriver@microchip.com. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/ocelot-core.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/mscc/phy-ocelot-serdes.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/mips/boot/dts/mscc/ocelot.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 2 top-level properties and 2 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mscc,vsc7514-serdes.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/mscc,vsc7514-serdes.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/nuvoton,ma35d1-usb2-phy.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/nuvoton,ma35d1-usb2-phy.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Nuvoton MA35D1 USB2 phy`, a USB PHY or USB dual-role PHY binding. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 1 compatible token: `nuvoton,ma35d1-usb2-phy`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `nuvoton,sys`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `nuvoton,sys`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#phy-cells`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `nuvoton,sys`. The binding acts as a simple provider node referenced by PHY consumers. Provider/cell contracts are `#phy-cells=const 0`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `nuvoton,ma35d1-usb2-phy`, `0`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Hui-Ping Chen <hpchen0nvt@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/nuvoton/phy-ma35d1-usb2.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/nuvoton,ma35d1-usb2-phy.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/nuvoton,ma35d1-usb2-phy.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

Each lane is controlled by a HW block referred to as a "pad" in the Tegra hardware documentation. Each such "pad" may control either one or multiple lanes, and thus contains any logic common to all its lanes. Each lane can be separately configured and powered up. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/tegra/xusb.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/nvidia/tegra194.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/nvidia/tegra234.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 10 top-level properties and 33 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/nvidia,tegra194-xusb-padctl.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/nvidia,tegra194-xusb-padctl.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `interrupts`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `#phy-cells`, `phy_type`, `dr_mode`, `vbus-supply`, `nvidia,has-legacy-mode`, `nvidia,is-wired`, `nvidia,has-utmi-pad-registers`, `nvidia,hssync-start-delay`, `nvidia,elastic-limit`, `nvidia,idle-wait-delay`, `nvidia,term-range-adj`, `nvidia,xcvr-setup`, `nvidia,xcvr-setup-use-fuses`, `nvidia,xcvr-lsfslew`, `nvidia,xcvr-lsrslew`, `nvidia,xcvr-hsslew`, `nvidia,hssquelch-level`, `nvidia,hsdiscon-level`, `nvidia,phy-reset-gpio`, `nvidia,pmc`. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `clocks` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `clock-names` binds reference, pipe, APB, PLL, or tracking clocks; `interrupts` routes PHY, pad controller, or link status IRQs; `resets` models reset controller lines; `reset-names` models reset controller lines; `#phy-cells` defines the phandle argument contract for PHY consumers; `vbus-supply` links analog/digital rails to regulator providers; `nvidia,phy-reset-gpio` describes control/status GPIO wiring. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/nvidia,tegra210-xusb-padctl.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/nvidia,tegra210-xusb-padctl.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `NVIDIA Tegra210 XUSB pad controller`, a XUSB pad controller / multi-lane PHY provider binding. The Tegra XUSB pad controller manages a set of I/O lanes (with differential signals) which connect directly to pins/pads on the SoC package. Each lane is controlled by a HW block referred to as a "pad" in the Tegra hardware documentation. Each such "pad" may control either one or multiple lanes, and thus contains any logic common to all its lanes. Each lane can be separately configured and powered up. Some of the lanes are high-speed lanes, which can be used for PCIe, SATA or super-speed USB. Other lanes are for various types of low-speed, full-speed or high-speed USB (such as UTMI, ULPI and HSIC). The XUSB pad controller contains a software-configurable mux that sits between the I/O controller ports (e.g. P... Numeric/item constraints include `minItems=1`, `maxItems=1`, `minItems=2`, `maxItems=4`, `minimum=0`, `maximum=15`, `minimum=1`, `maximum=16`; selected enum/const literals include `cdns,sierra-phy-t0`, `ti,sierra-phy-t0`, `1`, `0`, `sierra_reset`, `sierra_apb`, `serdes`, `cmn_refclk_dig_div`, `cmn_refclk1_dig_div`, `pll0_refclk`, `pll1_refclk`, `2`, `4`, `8`, `9`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Swapnil Jakhade <sjakhade@cadence.com>, Yuti Amonkar <yamonkar@cadence.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/cadence/phy-cadence-sierra.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/ti/k3-j721e-main.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 11 top-level properties and 15 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/phy-cadence-sierra.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/phy-cadence-sierra.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`, `maxItems=4`, `maxItems=5`, `minItems=2`, `maximum=3`; selected enum/const literals include `rockchip,rk3576-usbdp-phy`, `rockchip,rk3588-usbdp-phy`, `1`, `refclk`, `immortal`, `pclk`, `utmi`, `init`, `cmn`, `lane`, `pcs_apb`, `pma_apb`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Frank Wang <frank.wang@rock-chips.com>, Zhang Yubing <yubing.zhang@rock-chips.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/graph.yaml#/properties/port`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32-array`. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/phy/rockchip/phy-rockchip-usbdp.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3576.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3588-base.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3588-extra.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

**Risks:** Primary risks are PHY ABI drift and resource lookup mismatch. Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. Because this schema has 18 top-level properties and 18 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/phy-rockchip-usbdp.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/phy-rockchip-usbdp.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.

The STM32 USBPHYC block contains a dual port High Speed UTMI+ PHY and a UTMI switch. It controls PHY configuration and status, and the UTMI+ switch that selects either OTG or HOST controller for the second PHY port. It also sets PLL configuration. **Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/phy-tegra194-p2u.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `NVIDIA Tegra194 & Tegra234 P2U`, a PCIe PHY binding. Tegra194 has two PHY bricks namely HSIO (High Speed IO) and NVHS (NVIDIA High Speed) each interfacing with 12 and 8 P2U instances respectively. Tegra234 has three PHY bricks namely HSIO, NVHS and GBE (Gigabit Ethernet) each interfacing with 8, 8 and 8 P2U instances respectively. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Because this schema has 7 top-level properties and 7 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/qcom,dsi-phy-10nm.yaml` in a kernel tree with dt-schema installed and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/qcom,dsi-phy-10nm.yaml` for boards using these compatibles. Useful negative checks omit required clocks/resets/supplies, use invalid `#phy-cells`, bad child-node names, wrong lane `reg` values, or unsupported enum values. Runtime integration signals are successful driver probe, registered PHY providers with expected phandle arguments, consumers resolving `phys`, clocks and regulators balanced across suspend/resume, link bring-up for the relevant USB/PCIe/SATA/UFS/MIPI/SerDes protocol, and no new schema warnings for examples or in-tree DTS files.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/qcom,dsi-phy-10nm.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/qcom,dsi-phy-14nm.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/phy/qcom,dsi-phy-14nm.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm Display DSI 14nm PHY`, a MIPI D-PHY, CSI, or DSI PHY binding. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its role is to make board DTS/DTB nodes describe PHY blocks, lanes, pads, ports, supplies, clocks, resets, and phandle provider contracts in the form the matching Linux PHY or controller driver expects.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The exported API is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `patternProperties`, local definitions, `required`, examples, and composition constraints. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 6 compatible tokens: `qcom,dsi-phy-14nm`, `qcom,dsi-phy-14nm-2290`, `qcom,dsi-phy-14nm-660`, `qcom,dsi-phy-14nm-8953`, `qcom,sm6125-dsi-phy-14nm`, `qcom,sm6150-dsi-phy-14nm`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `vcca-supply`, `power-domains`, `required-opps`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`. The binding acts as a platform, syscon, or bus child node selected by `reg`. Provider/cell contracts are no explicit provider cell contract discovered. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, syscon offsets, port numbers, or bus addresses; `vcca-supply` links analog/digital rails to regulator providers; `power-domains` links the PHY to runtime PM domain providers. Patterned child schemas are none; nested topology or child names discovered from properties are no explicit nested pad/lane/port names discovered. Local reusable definitions are none. Numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`; selected enum/const literals include `qcom,dsi-phy-14nm`, `qcom,dsi-phy-14nm-2290`, `qcom,dsi-phy-14nm-660`, `qcom,dsi-phy-14nm-8953`, `qcom,sm6125-dsi-phy-14nm`, `qcom,sm6150-dsi-phy-14nm`, `dsi_phy`, `dsi_phy_lane`, `dsi_pll`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates it against the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` dependencies, applies `properties` and `patternProperties`, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/conditional branches, checks `required` lists and item counts, compiles examples, and enforces the closure rule. At boot the YAML is not interpreted; firmware supplies a compiled DTB node, the kernel matches `compatible` in the relevant OF table, and the PHY/provider driver reads `reg`, clocks, resets, supplies, child lanes/ports, and `#phy-cells` to register PHYs. Consumer controllers later resolve `phys` phandles and invoke the generic PHY framework for init, power-on, mode setting, calibration, and power-off.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file stores no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, valid enum values, provider cell counts, child-node naming, lane or port numbering, and allowed topology. Runtime state such as PLL lock, link training, lane muxing, PHY mode, impedance tuning, analog calibration, reset sequencing, and power transitions lives in the matching kernel driver and hardware registers; this schema only constrains how those resources and modes are represented in firmware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Krishna Manikandan <quic_mkrishn@quicinc.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `qcom,dsi-phy-common.yaml#`. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points are Devicetree common schemas, the Linux generic PHY framework, controller bindings that consume `phys`/`phy-names`, platform/MMIO or syscon buses, regulator supplies, reset controllers, clock providers, GPIO descriptors, power domains, and SoC-specific lane mux drivers. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/gpu/drm/msm/dsi/phy/dsi_phy.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/agatti.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8953.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8996.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sdm630.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sdm660.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sm6115.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sm6125.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/talos.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for validation.

Removing or reordering compatible fallbacks can break legacy DTBs; changing `#phy-cells`, child `reg` numbering, lane names, or port names can break consumers that already reference the provider; renaming clocks, resets, supplies, GPIOs, or power domains can break driver probe even when the hardware block is unchanged. Multi-protocol SerDes and pad-controller schemas are especially sensitive because lane mux enums, protocol names, and companion USB2/USB3 or PCIe/SATA relationships must align with driver tables and board wiring. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Because this schema has 6 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, broad edits should be checked against every compatible variant, examples, and existing DTS users.
